System.CommandLine;\nusing System.Text.Json;\nusing Guardrails.Cli.Ui;\nusing Guardrails.Core.Execution;\nusing Guardrails.Core.Journal;\nusing Guardrails.Core.State;\nusing Spectre.Console;\n\nnamespace Guardrails.Cli.Commands;\n\n/// <summary>\n/// <c>guardrails run [folder] [--fresh] [--no-ui]</c> — validate then execute the plan\n/// DAG (parallel, retry-aware, resume-aware). <c>--fresh</c> wipes runtime state first\n/// (SSOT §6.1). Live Spectre progress when interactive; plain lines otherwise. Exit codes\n/// per SSOT §7: 0 green, 1 error, 2 needs-human/failed, 3 cancelled. Defaults to the\n/// current directory when the folder is omitted.\n/// </summary>\npublic static class RunCommand\n{\n    public static Command Create(IConsoleIo io)\n    {\n        var folderArgument = FolderArgument.Create();\n\n        var freshOption = new Option<bool>(\"--fresh\")\n        {\n            Description = \"Delete runtime state (run.json, state.json, logs) and re-seed before running.\"\n        };\n\n        var noUiOption = new Option<bool>(\"--no-ui\")\n        {\n            Description = \"Plain line-by-line output instead of the live progress table.\"\n        };\n\n        var dryRunOption = new Option<bool>(\"--dry-run\")\n        {\n            Description = \"Validate and preview waves + per-task resolution + resume skips, then exit 0 without running or touching state.\"\n        };\n\n        var noLogServerOption = new Option<bool>(\"--no-log-server\")\n        {\n            Description = \"Do not start the local log server / clickable per-task log links (for headless or CI use).\"\n        };\n\n        var logPortOption = new Option<int>(\"--log-port\")\n        {\n            Description = \"Port for the local log server (default 0 = an automatically chosen free port). Bound to localhost only.\"\n        };\n\n        var mergeOnSuccessOption = new Option<bool>(\"--merge-on-success\")\n        {\n            Description = \"On a wholly-green run, merge the plan branch into your original branch at run end (SSOT §5.3). Forces mergeOnSuccess on regardless of guardrails.json.\"\n        };\n\n        var revalidateTaskOption = new Option<string?>(\"--revalidate-task\")\n        {\n            Description = \"Re-validate-only (issue #102): run ONLY this task's guardrails against the current workspace, spawning NO agent attempt — for confirming a hand-fix to a needs-human task. On pass the task is marked succeeded; serial mode only.\"\n        };\n\n        var skipReviewCheckOption = new Option<bool>(\"--skip-review-check\")\n        {\n            Description = \"Suppress the warning when the plan hasn't been through /guardrails-review (or has changed since) (SSOT §13, issue #79).\"\n        };\n\n        var command = new Command(\"run\", \"Run a plan folder's task DAG to green (parallel; resume-aware).\");\n        command.Add(folderArgument);\n        command.Add(freshOption);\n        command.Add(noUiOption);\n        command.Add(dryRunOption);\n        command.Add(noLogServerOption);\n        command.Add(logPortOption);\n        command.Add(mergeOnSuccessOption);\n        command.Add(revalidateTaskOption);\n        command.Add(skipReviewCheckOption);\n\n        command.SetAction(async (parseResult, cancellationToken) =>\n        {\n            string folder = FolderArgument.ResolveAndAnnounce(parseResult.GetValue(folderArgument), io.Out);\n            bool fresh = parseResult.GetValue(freshOption);\n            bool noUi = parseResult.GetValue(noUiOption);\n            bool dryRun = parseResult.GetValue(dryRunOption);\n            bool noLogServer = parseResult.GetValue(noLogServerOption);\n            int logPort = parseResult.GetValue(logPortOption);\n            bool mergeOnSuccess = parseResult.GetValue(mergeOnSuccessOption);\n            string? revalidateTask = parseResult.GetValue(revalidateTaskOption);\n            bool skipReviewCheck = parseResult.GetValue(skipReviewCheckOption);\n\n            // Re-validate-only (issue #102) is a single-task verification, not a run: it spawns no\n            // agent attempt and ignores the run-shaped flags. Reject the combinations that would\n            // otherwise silently no-op (or, for --fresh, destroy the very state being verified).\n            if (!string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(revalidateTask))\n            {\n                if (fresh || dryRun)\n                {\n                    io.Out.WriteLine(\"--revalidate-task cannot be combined with --fresh or --dry-run.\");\n                    return ExitCodes.HarnessError;\n                }\n\n                return await Revalidate.ExecuteAsync(folder, revalidateTask, io, cancellationToken).ConfigureAwait(false);\n            }\n\n            if (dryRun)\n            {\n                return DryRun.Execute(folder, io, skipReviewCheck);\n            }\n\n            return await RunAsync(folder, fresh, noUi, noLogServer, logPort, mergeOnSuccess, skipReviewCheck, io, cancellationToken).ConfigureAwait(false);\n        });\n\n        return command;\n    }\n\n    private static async Task<int> RunAsync(\n        string folder, bool fresh, bool noUi, bool noLogServer, int logPort, bool mergeOnSuccess, bool skipReviewCheck, IConsoleIo io, CancellationToken cancellationToken)\n    {\n        PlanProbe.Result probe = PlanProbe.LoadAndValidate(folder);\n        if (probe.HasErrors || probe.Plan is null)\n        {\n            PlanProbe.PrintDiagnostics(probe.Diagnostics, io.Out);\n            io.Out.WriteLine(\"\\nValidation failed; nothing was run.\");\n            return ExitCodes.HarnessError;\n        }\n\n        // Review-marker nudge (warn, never block — SSOT §13, issue #79): if the plan hasn't been\n        // through /guardrails-review (or has changed since), print a one-line warning before running,\n        // unless --skip-review-check. Reuses the same deterministic evaluation as `validate`.\n        WarnIfUnreviewed(probe.Plan, skipReviewCheck, io);\n\n        // --merge-on-success forces end-of-run delivery on (SSOT §5.3 / §2: \"CLI --merge-on-success\n        // overrides\"). The flag only augments — it can turn the config value on, never off — so a\n        // plan that already set mergeOnSuccess: true is unaffected.\n        if (mergeOnSuccess && !probe.Plan.Config.MergeOnSuccess)\n        {\n            probe = probe with { Plan = probe.Plan with { Config = probe.Plan.Config with { MergeOnSuccess = true } } };\n        }\n\n        if (fresh)\n        {\n            RunReset.Fresh(probe.Plan.PlanDirectory);\n            io.Out.WriteLine(\"Fresh run: runtime state cleared and re-seeded.\\n\");\n        }\n\n        bool live = !noUi && AnsiConsole.Profile.Capabilities.Interactive && !Console.IsOutputRedirected;\n\n        // Resolve the run's id up-front so the live log server and the post-mortem links target the\n        // correct logs/<runId>/ tree (SSOT §8/§12). LoadOrCreate is idempotent: it creates run.json\n        // here (or reads it on resume), and the Scheduler's own LoadOrCreate then reads the SAME\n        // run.json — so this runId matches the one the executor writes attempt logs under.\n        RunJournal journal = RunJournal.LoadOrCreate(probe.Plan);\n        string runId = journal.Document.RunId;\n\n        // Pre-DAG plan-preflight phase (SSOT §7, deliverable 3): evaluate <plan>/preflights/ ONCE,\n        // BEFORE the Scheduler builds any wave, against the run's starting bytes. A red preflight halts\n        // HERE — no task runs, zero tokens spent — journaled as planPreflights.status =\n        // plan-preflight-failed (a top-level section OUTSIDE tasks{}). A passed marker whose planHash\n        // still matches the current plan is SKIPPED on resume rather than re-evaluated (the B1 fix).\n        // Issue #240: this phase (and the terminal one below) previously ran entirely silently on\n        // success — no row in the live table (its lifetime doesn't even span these phases, #240's own\n        // investigation), no console line at all. Bracket with plain WriteLines, guarded on the plan\n        // actually declaring this folder (EvaluateAsync itself no-ops for free when it doesn't, so\n        // printing \"running...\" then would be misleading noise for a plan that never opted in).\n        bool hasPlanPreflights = probe.Plan.PlanPreflights.Count > 0;\n        if (hasPlanPreflights)\n        {\n            io.Out.WriteLine(\"Full Flight Checks: running...\");\n        }\n\n        bool preflightsPassed = await PlanPreflightPhase\n            .EvaluateAsync(probe.Plan, journal, new ProcessRunner(), cancellationToken)\n            .ConfigureAwait(false);\n\n        if (hasPlanPreflights && preflightsPassed)\n        {\n            io.Out.WriteLine(\"Full Flight Checks: passed.\");\n        }\n\n        if (!preflightsPassed)\n        {\n            io.Out.WriteLine();\n            io.Out.WriteLine(\"Plan preflight FAILED — halting before scheduling any task (SSOT §7 planPreflights).\");\n            io.Out.WriteLine($\"  See {RunJournal.PathFor(probe.Plan.PlanDirectory)} (\\\"planPreflights\\\") for the failed check(s).\");\n            return ExitCodes.TaskFailed;\n        }\n\n        // The log server is a companion to the live table: start it only in the interactive path\n        // (nobody clicks links in CI / redirected output), and never let a binding failure abort\n        // the run — TryStart returns null and prints one warning.\n        LogServer? logServer = (live && !noLogServer)\n            ? LogServer.TryStart(probe.Plan.PlanDirectory, runId, probe.Plan.Tasks, logPort, io.Out)\n            : null;\n\n        try\n        {\n            if (logServer is not null)\n            {\n                // The canonical \"all tasks\" page is the static index file (printed by\n                // PrintStaticIndexLink below); this http server is just the tailing backend that the\n                // static index links a RUNNING task to (issue #143). De-emphasised accordingly.\n                io.Out.WriteLine($\"Live tailing server (active tasks): {logServer.BaseUrl}\\n\");\n            }\n\n            Func<string, string?>? logUrlForTask = logServer is null ? null : logServer.UrlForTask;\n\n            // The on-the-fly static site (issue #141 item 2) is written for BOTH the live and the\n            // --no-ui paths — a file:// \"all tasks\" page that updates as tasks settle, useful headless\n            // or interactive. It lives under logs/<runId>/, the same tree the executor writes attempts\n            // into and the live server serves. The inner observer (live table or plain console) is\n            // wrapped so the site is rewritten after each forwarded event.\n            string logsRoot = Path.Combine(probe.Plan.PlanDirectory, \"logs\", runId);\n\n            RunReport report;\n            if (live)\n            {\n                // Write the initial all-pending index AND print its link BEFORE constructing\n                // LiveRunObserver — its ctor starts the Spectre AnsiConsole.Live region, and any\n                // console write into an active Live region corrupts the table (#145 Bug 1). So the\n                // static-index write + its link must precede the live region.\n                OnTheFlyLogSiteObserver.WriteInitialIndex(logsRoot, runId, probe.Plan.Tasks, logUrlForTask);\n                PrintStaticIndexLink(logsRoot, io);    // \"all tasks\" page link at run START\n\n                await using var liveObserver = new LiveRunObserver(probe.Plan.Tasks, logUrlForTask, probe.Plan.PlanDirectory, runId);\n                var siteObserver = new OnTheFlyLogSiteObserver(liveObserver, logsRoot, runId, probe.Plan.Tasks, logUrlForTask);\n                report = await ExecuteAsync(probe.Plan, siteObserver, cancellationToken).ConfigureAwait(false);\n            }\n            else\n            {\n                var siteObserver = new OnTheFlyLogSiteObserver(\n                    new ConsoleRunObserver(io.Out), logsRoot, runId, probe.Plan.Tasks, logUrlForTask);\n                siteObserver.WriteInitialIndex();\n                PrintStaticIndexLink(logsRoot, io);\n                report = await ExecuteAsync(probe.Plan, siteObserver, cancellationToken).ConfigureAwait(false);\n            }\n\n            // Terminal plan-guardrail phase (SSOT §7/§7.1, deliverable 4): evaluate <plan>/guardrails/\n            // ONCE, AFTER the DAG drains wholly green, against the merged plan-branch HEAD — replacing\n            // the retired integrationGate task-kind's terminal role (Scheduler.cs skips that legacy path\n            // whenever the plan declares this folder, SSOT §3.3). No-op (true) when the DAG did not\n            // fully succeed this run/resume, or the plan has no <plan>/guardrails/ folder at all.\n            // B2(b) terminal-only resume falls out for free: a resume where every task is already\n            // succeeded drains the DAG with nothing left to do (report.AllSucceeded stays true, no\n            // attempt burned), so this phase unconditionally re-fires against the current HEAD.\n            // Issue #240: same silent-on-success gap as Full Flight Checks above. This phase is only\n            // ever actually invoked when the DAG settled green AND the plan declares this folder (the\n            // `!report.AllSucceeded ||` short-circuit means EvaluateAsync is never called at all\n            // otherwise) — gate the bracketing lines on exactly that, or \"Terminal Gate: running...\"\n            // would misleadingly print for a run that failed before ever reaching this phase.\n            bool hasPlanGuardrails = probe.Plan.PlanGuardrails.Count > 0;\n            bool willEvaluateTerminalGate = report.AllSucceeded && hasPlanGuardrails;\n            if (willEvaluateTerminalGate)\n            {\n                io.Out.WriteLine(\"Terminal Gate: running...\");\n            }\n\n            bool planGuardrailsPassed = !report.AllSucceeded\n                || await PlanGuardrailPhase.EvaluateAsync(probe.Plan, new ProcessRunner(), cancellationToken)\n                    .ConfigureAwait(false);\n\n            if (willEvaluateTerminalGate && planGuardrailsPassed)\n            {\n                io.Out.WriteLine(\"Terminal Gate: passed.\");\n            }\n\n            int exitCode = Finish(report, probe.Plan, runId, io);\n            if (report.AllSucceeded && !planGuardrailsPassed)\n            {\n                PrintTerminalGateFailure(probe.Plan.PlanDirectory, io);\n                return ExitCodes.TaskFailed;\n            }\n\n            return exitCode;\n        }\n        finally\n        {\n            if (logServer is not null)\n            {\n                await logServer.DisposeAsync().ConfigureAwait(false);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Print the review-marker nudge (GR2025, WARNING — SSOT §13, issue #79) when the plan is\n    /// missing/stale a <c>/guardrails-review</c> marker, unless <paramref name=\"skipReviewCheck\"/>.\n    /// Warn, never block — the run proceeds regardless. Shared by <c>run</c> and <c>--dry-run</c>.\n    /// </summary>\n    public static void WarnIfUnreviewed(Core.Model.PlanDefinition plan, bool skipReviewCheck, IConsoleIo io)\n    {\n        if (skipReviewCheck)\n        {\n            return;\n        }\n\n        if (Core.Loading.PlanValidator.ReviewMarkerDiagnostic(plan) is { } nudge)\n        {\n            io.Out.WriteLine(nudge.ToString());\n            io.Out.WriteLine();\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>Print the summary and map the report to the process exit code (SSOT §7).</summary>\n    private static int Finish(RunReport report, Core.Model.PlanDefinition plan, string runId, IConsoleIo io)\n    {\n        string planDirectory = plan.PlanDirectory;\n        string logsRoot = Path.Combine(planDirectory, \"logs\", runId);\n\n        // Write the DURABLE final site (issue #141 item 2): all-static links, NO meta-refresh, every\n        // task page — so the artifact left on disk is complete and self-contained (identical to\n        // `logs --export`). The during-run writer left a refreshing index with live links; this\n        // replaces it. Best-effort: a render hiccup must never change the run's exit code.\n        WriteDurableFinalSite(logsRoot, plan, planDirectory);\n\n        PrintSummary(report, planDirectory, runId, io);\n\n        // The \"all tasks\" static page link at run END (alongside the post-mortem logs pointer).\n        PrintStaticIndexLink(logsRoot, io);\n\n        // Issue #150 — honest halt for an infrastructure fault. The scheduler returned an ABORTED\n        // report instead of throwing; render a one-line diagnostic + remedy as the headline, write\n        // the FULL exception to the run logs (a dev tool keeps the detail, just not as the headline),\n        // and exit non-zero — never a raw unhandled stack trace as the headline.\n        if (report.Abort is { } abort)\n        {\n            WriteAbortDetailToLogs(logsRoot, abort);\n            io.Out.WriteLine();\n            io.Out.WriteLine($\"RUN ABORTED: {abort.Headline}\");\n            io.Out.WriteLine($\"  {abort.Remedy}\");\n            io.Out.WriteLine($\"  Full fault detail written to {Path.GetFullPath(Path.Combine(logsRoot, \"abort.log\"))}\");\n            return ExitCodes.HarnessError;\n        }\n\n        if (report.Cancelled)\n        {\n            return ExitCodes.Cancelled;\n        }\n\n        // Issue #150 — a wholly-green run whose end-of-run delivery to the user's branch was HALTED\n        // (a git hook rejected the user-facing merge, a conflict, or a dirty user tree) is NOT a\n        // clean success: the work is durable on the plan branch, but the user must act. Render the\n        // actionable message and exit non-zero. A FastForwarded/Merged delivery, or no mergeOnSuccess\n        // at all (null), leaves the success verdict untouched.\n        if (report.AllSucceeded\n            && report.MergeOnSuccessOutcome is { } mergeOutcome\n            && mergeOutcome is not (MergeOnSuccessResult.FastForwarded or MergeOnSuccessResult.Merged))\n        {\n            PrintMergeOnSuccessHalt(report, plan, mergeOutcome, io);\n            return ExitCodes.TaskFailed;\n        }\n\n        return report.AllSucceeded ? ExitCodes.Success : ExitCodes.TaskFailed;\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Print the terminal plan-guardrail gate failure (D4). Read the failed checks (name + reason) that\n    /// <see cref=\"PlanGuardrailPhase\"/> journaled into <c>planGuardrails.failedChecks</c> and surface each\n    /// one INLINE — so a terminal halt is as legible as the legacy per-task gate (which listed its failed\n    /// guardrails in the summary), instead of a bare \"see planGuardrails in run.json\" pointer that forces\n    /// the user to open the journal. Mirrors the shape of the NEEDS HUMAN block. Best-effort: a journal\n    /// read hiccup falls back to the generic pointer rather than throwing (the exit code is unaffected).\n    /// <para>\n    /// It ALSO surfaces the #175 merge-collision hint (SSOT §3.3, issue #205) that\n    /// <see cref=\"PlanGuardrailPhase\"/> journals into <c>planGuardrails.collisionHint</c> when ≥2 tasks\n    /// have overlapping <c>writeScope</c> on a shared file — the same attribution the legacy per-task gate\n    /// carried in its summary, ported onto the terminal phase.\n    /// </para>\n    /// </summary>\n    private static void PrintTerminalGateFailure(string planDirectory, IConsoleIo io)\n    {\n        TextWriter output = io.Out;\n        string journalPath = RunJournal.PathFor(planDirectory);\n\n        output.WriteLine();\n        output.WriteLine(\"Plan guardrail gate FAILED on the merged HEAD — terminal halt (SSOT §7 planGuardrails).\");\n\n        PlanGuardrailsSection? section = TryReadPlanGuardrailSection(journalPath);\n        IReadOnlyList<FailedGuardrail> failedChecks = section?.FailedChecks ?? [];\n        if (failedChecks.Count > 0)\n        {\n            foreach (FailedGuardrail check in failedChecks)\n            {\n                output.WriteLine($\"  FAILED: {check.Name} — {check.Reason}\");\n            }\n            output.WriteLine($\"  (full detail in {journalPath} under \\\"planGuardrails\\\")\");\n        }\n        else\n        {\n            // No structured checks readable (older/absent section, or a read hiccup): the prior pointer.\n            output.WriteLine($\"  See {journalPath} (\\\"planGuardrails\\\") for the failed check(s).\");\n        }\n\n        // #175/#205 merge-collision attribution — advisory, only present when writeScopes overlap.\n        if (section?.CollisionHint is { Length: > 0 } collisionHint)\n        {\n            output.WriteLine($\"  {collisionHint}\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Read the terminal gate's <c>planGuardrails</c> section (failed checks + the #175 collision hint)\n    /// from the persisted journal. Returns null when the section is absent/passed or the journal cannot be\n    /// read — the caller then falls back to the generic pointer.\n    /// </summary>\n    private static PlanGuardrailsSection? TryReadPlanGuardrailSection(string journalPath)\n    {\n        try\n        {\n            if (!File.Exists(journalPath))\n            {\n                return null;\n            }\n\n            JournalDocument document = JournalReader.Read(journalPath);\n            return document.PlanGuardrails;\n        }\n        catch (IOException)\n        {\n            return null;\n        }\n        catch (UnauthorizedAccessException)\n        {\n            return null;\n        }\n        catch (JsonException)\n        {\n            return null;\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Render the actionable end-of-run delivery halt (issue #150). The plan branch carries all the\n    /// (verified) work; only the optional merge back into the user's branch was refused. For a hook\n    /// rejection the user's own hook stderr (<see cref=\"RunReport.MergeOnSuccessDetail\"/>) is shown\n    /// verbatim so they see exactly why and can resolve it or disable the hook for the merge.\n    /// </summary>\n    private static void PrintMergeOnSuccessHalt(\n        RunReport report, Core.Model.PlanDefinition plan, MergeOnSuccessResult outcome, IConsoleIo io)\n    {\n        TextWriter output = io.Out;\n        string planBranch = $\"guardrails/{Path.GetFileName(plan.PlanDirectory)}\";\n\n        output.WriteLine();\n        switch (outcome)\n        {\n            case MergeOnSuccessResult.HookRejected:\n                output.WriteLine(\n                    $\"All tasks passed and are on branch `{planBranch}`. The final merge into your \" +\n                    \"branch was rejected by your git hook:\");\n                if (!string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(report.MergeOnSuccessDetail))\n                {\n                    output.WriteLine($\"  {report.MergeOnSuccessDetail}\");\n                }\n                output.WriteLine(\n                    \"  Resolve and merge manually, or disable the hook for the merge. Your branch is \" +\n                    \"unchanged (the merge was aborted).\");\n                break;\n\n            case MergeOnSuccessResult.Conflict:\n                output.WriteLine(\n                    $\"All tasks passed and are on branch `{planBranch}`. The final merge into your \" +\n                    \"branch CONFLICTED with a change made on your branch during the run; AI-merge is \" +\n                    \"withheld here (SSOT §5.3). Your branch is unchanged — merge `\" + planBranch +\n                    \"` manually.\");\n                break;\n\n            case MergeOnSuccessResult.DirtyWorkingTree:\n                output.WriteLine(\n                    $\"All tasks passed and are on branch `{planBranch}`. The final merge into your \" +\n                    \"branch was refused because your working tree has uncommitted changes. Commit or \" +\n                    \"stash them, then merge `\" + planBranch + \"` manually.\");\n                break;\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Write an aborted run's FULL fault detail (issue #150) to <c>logs/&lt;runId&gt;/abort.log</c> so\n    /// the console headline stays a one-liner while the dev keeps the whole exception. Best-effort —\n    /// a logs-tree write hiccup must never change the run's exit code or mask the abort.\n    /// </summary>\n    private static void WriteAbortDetailToLogs(string logsRoot, Core.Execution.RunAbort abort)\n    {\n        try\n        {\n            Directory.CreateDirectory(logsRoot);\n            File.WriteAllText(\n                Path.Combine(logsRoot, \"abort.log\"),\n                $\"{abort.Headline}\\n\\n{abort.Remedy}\\n\\n--- full fault detail ---\\n{abort.Detail}\\n\");\n        }\n        catch (IOException) { /* best-effort */ }\n        catch (UnauthorizedAccessException) { /* best-effort */ }\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Render the durable, self-contained static site (all-static links, no refresh, every task page)\n    /// at run end via <see cref=\"LogSiteRenderer.ExportSite\"/>, reading the freshly-persisted journal\n    /// for per-task status. No-op (and never throws) when the journal is absent — a fully-resumed /\n    /// all-skipped run writes no logs, so there is nothing to render.\n    /// </summary>\n    private static void WriteDurableFinalSite(string logsRoot, Core.Model.PlanDefinition plan, string planDirectory)\n    {\n        string journalPath = RunJournal.PathFor(planDirectory);\n        if (!File.Exists(journalPath) || !Directory.Exists(logsRoot))\n        {\n            return;\n        }\n\n        try\n        {\n            JournalDocument document = JournalReader.Read(journalPath);\n            LogSiteRenderer.ExportSite(logsRoot, plan.Tasks, document);\n        }\n        catch (IOException)\n        {\n            // Best-effort durable site — a transient lock must never flip the run's exit code.\n        }\n        catch (UnauthorizedAccessException)\n        {\n            // ditto — a logs-tree permission hiccup is not a run failure.\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Print a clickable <c>file://</c> link to the run's static \"all tasks\" index\n    /// (<c>logs/&lt;runId&gt;/index.html</c>, issue #141 item 2) — the during-run refreshing page at run\n    /// start, the durable page at run end. Emits an OSC 8 hyperlink only when the terminal can render\n    /// one (matching the post-mortem pointer's gate); otherwise the plain absolute path, which is\n    /// copy-pasteable. No-op when the index does not exist (nothing was rendered).\n    /// </summary>\n    private static void PrintStaticIndexLink(string logsRoot, IConsoleIo io)\n    {\n        string indexPath = Path.GetFullPath(Path.Combine(logsRoot, \"index.html\"));\n        if (!File.Exists(indexPath))\n        {\n            return;\n        }\n\n        bool linkable = !Console.IsOutputRedirected && AnsiConsole.Profile.Capabilities.Links;\n        io.Out.WriteLine($\"All tasks (static log site): {Hyperlink(indexPath, linkable)}\");\n    }\n\n    private static Task<RunReport> ExecuteAsync(\n        Core.Model.PlanDefinition plan,\n        IRunObserver observer,\n        CancellationToken cancellationToken)\n    {\n        Scheduler scheduler = SchedulerFactory.Create(plan, new ProcessRunner(), new PathExecutableProbe(), observer);\n        return scheduler.RunAsync(plan, cancellationToken);\n    }\n\n    private static void PrintSummary(RunReport report, string planDirectory, string runId, IConsoleIo io)\n    {\n        TextWriter output = io.Out;\n\n        output.WriteLine(\"Summary\");\n        output.WriteLine(\"-------\");\n        foreach (TaskResult result in report.Tasks)\n        {\n            output.WriteLine($\"  {StatusLabel(result.Outcome),-16} {result.TaskId,-32} {result.Summary}\");\n        }\n\n        int green = report.Tasks.Count(t => t.IsGreen);\n        output.WriteLine();\n        output.WriteLine(report.Cancelled\n            ? $\"Run CANCELLED — {green}/{report.Tasks.Count} task(s) green; in-flight tasks journaled pending. Re-run to resume.\"\n            : $\"{green}/{report.Tasks.Count} task(s) green (succeeded or skipped).\");\n\n        PrintTotalCost(planDirectory, output);\n\n        // Post-mortem pointer for EVERY task, not just failures: a green task whose guardrails\n        // turned out too weak is reviewed from the same on-disk logs (action output, guardrail\n        // stdout, feedback per attempt). The link target is the ABSOLUTE logs/<runId>/ root so it is\n        // clickable (issue #59); the <task-id>/attempt-N/ layout follows as guidance text. The\n        // per-attempt artifacts live under logs/<runId>/ (SSOT §8), NOT the pre-plan-08 state/logs/.\n        string logsRoot = Path.GetFullPath(Path.Combine(planDirectory, \"logs\", runId));\n        string sep = Path.DirectorySeparatorChar.ToString();\n        // Emit a clickable OSC 8 link only when the terminal can actually render one — matching the\n        // live table's gate. Redirection alone is too weak: a non-redirected but hyperlink-incapable\n        // TTY would get raw escape bytes as visible garbage, which Spectre's link capability check\n        // avoids. Also require the target to exist — a full-resume/all-skipped run writes no logs, so\n        // don't advertise a link that 404s. When not linkable the plain absolute path still serves as\n        // copy-pasteable guidance and fixes the #59 regression (it was relative with literal placeholders).\n        bool linkable = !Console.IsOutputRedirected\n                        && AnsiConsole.Profile.Capabilities.Links\n                        && Directory.Exists(logsRoot);\n        output.WriteLine();\n        output.WriteLine($\"Logs (post-mortem any task — pass or fail): {Hyperlink(logsRoot, linkable)}\");\n        output.WriteLine($\"  each task's attempts are under <task-id>{sep}attempt-N{sep}\");\n\n        PrintNeedsHumanSections(report, logsRoot, output);\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Print the post-run NEEDS HUMAN sections, resolving each task's triage diagnosis from the\n    /// on-disk <c>triage.json</c> sidecar in its task-level log dir. Thin production wrapper over the\n    /// pure <see cref=\"RenderNeedsHumanSections\"/> (which is unit-tested with an injected resolver).\n    /// </summary>\n    private static void PrintNeedsHumanSections(RunReport report, string logsRoot, TextWriter output) =>\n        RenderNeedsHumanSections(\n            report.Tasks, logsRoot, output,\n            taskLogDir => TriageSummaryReader.TryRead(taskLogDir));\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Render the post-run NEEDS HUMAN sections (issue #163): per failed/needs-human task, surface the\n    /// AI triage root-cause CATEGORY + one-line diagnosis (and the drafted GH-issue title when present)\n    /// directly in the console — so the user does not open each <c>feedback.md</c>. When several tasks\n    /// share a diagnosis category the repeat is annotated (\"same root cause as …\") so one fix resolving\n    /// several failures is obvious at a glance. A task with no structured triage (unstructured or failed\n    /// — <paramref name=\"triageFor\"/> returns null) renders the prior shape, unchanged. The leading line\n    /// stays parseable: <c>NEEDS HUMAN: &lt;task-id&gt; — &lt;summary&gt;</c>.\n    /// <para>\n    /// Pure (no IO) — the triage lookup is injected as <paramref name=\"triageFor\"/> (the task-level log\n    /// dir → <see cref=\"TriageSummary\"/>), so the production path reads the sidecar and tests inject a\n    /// fake. Public for the same reason <see cref=\"Hyperlink\"/> is: the Cli assembly ships no\n    /// InternalsVisibleTo.\n    /// </para>\n    /// </summary>\n    public static void RenderNeedsHumanSections(\n        IReadOnlyList<TaskResult> tasks,\n        string logsRoot,\n        TextWriter output,\n        Func<string, TriageSummary?> triageFor)\n    {\n        // Issue #190: a rate-limited task is NOT \"fix the action or guardrails\" — it is a healthy task\n        // waiting on a provider-side limit. Give it its own section with the correct advice (\"re-run\n        // later\") instead of folding it into the generic NEEDS HUMAN loop below, whose guidance would\n        // mislead an operator into debugging a task that isn't broken.\n        foreach (TaskResult rateLimited in tasks.Where(t => t.Outcome is TaskOutcome.RateLimited))\n        {\n            output.WriteLine();\n            output.WriteLine($\"RATE LIMITED: {rateLimited.TaskId} — {rateLimited.Summary}\");\n            output.WriteLine(\"  Not a task defect — a provider-side limit did not clear in time. Re-run this plan\");\n            output.WriteLine(\"  later (the harness resumes from here), or raise transientPauseBudgetSeconds.\");\n        }\n\n        // First task id seen per category, so a later same-category task can point back to it.\n        var firstTaskForCategory = new Dictionary<string, string>(StringComparer.Ordinal);\n\n        foreach (TaskResult needsHuman in tasks.Where(t =>\n                     t.Outcome is TaskOutcome.ActionFailed or TaskOutcome.GuardrailFailed\n                         or TaskOutcome.InvalidFragment or TaskOutcome.NeedsHuman))\n        {\n            output.WriteLine();\n            output.WriteLine($\"NEEDS HUMAN: {needsHuman.TaskId} — {needsHuman.Summary}\");\n\n            string taskLogDir = Path.Combine(logsRoot, needsHuman.TaskId);\n            if (triageFor(taskLogDir) is { } triage)\n            {\n                string rootCause = string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(triage.OneLine)\n                    ? $\"Root cause [{triage.Diagnosis}]\"\n                    : $\"Root cause [{triage.Diagnosis}]: {triage.OneLine}\";\n\n                // Group annotation: a second-or-later task in the same category points back to the\n                // first, making \"one fix resolves several failures\" visible without opening files.\n                if (firstTaskForCategory.TryGetValue(triage.Diagnosis, out string? firstTask))\n                {\n                    rootCause += $\" (same root cause as {firstTask})\";\n                }\n                else\n                {\n                    firstTaskForCategory[triage.Diagnosis] = needsHuman.TaskId;\n                }\n\n                output.WriteLine($\"  {rootCause}\");\n                if (!string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(triage.GhIssueTitle)\n                    && !string.Equals(triage.GhIssueTitle, triage.OneLine, StringComparison.Ordinal))\n                {\n                    output.WriteLine($\"  Draft GH issue: {triage.GhIssueTitle}\");\n                }\n            }\n\n            output.WriteLine($\"  Inspect {taskLogDir}{Path.DirectorySeparatorChar} (latest attempt's feedback.md has the full failure detail),\");\n            output.WriteLine(\"  fix the action or guardrails, then re-run to resume.\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Print the run-level cost line (SSOT §7 <c>costUsd</c>) from the freshly-persisted\n    /// journal. Omitted when no attempt recorded a cost, so deterministic-only plans stay\n    /// noise-free.\n    /// </summary>\n    private static void PrintTotalCost(string planDirectory, TextWriter output)\n    {\n        string journalPath = RunJournal.PathFor(planDirectory);\n        if (!File.Exists(journalPath))\n        {\n            return;\n        }\n\n        JournalDocument document = JournalReader.Read(journalPath);\n        if (JournalCost.Total(document) is { } total)\n        {\n            output.WriteLine($\"Total prompt cost: ${total:F4}\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Render <paramref name=\"absolutePath\"/> as an OSC 8 hyperlink (clickable in capable terminals —\n    /// Windows Terminal, VS Code, iTerm2) targeting its <c>file://</c> URI, mirroring the per-task\n    /// links in the live table. When <paramref name=\"enabled\"/> is false — output redirected, the\n    /// terminal can't render hyperlinks, or the target doesn't exist — the escape sequence would be\n    /// noise, so emit the plain absolute path instead. The caller owns the capability decision so this\n    /// stays a pure, testable function. Public (not private) because the Cli assembly ships no\n    /// InternalsVisibleTo — same rationale as <see cref=\"LogsCommand\"/>'s test seams.\n    /// </summary>\n    public static string Hyperlink(string absolutePath, bool enabled)\n    {\n        if (!enabled)\n        {\n            return absolutePath;\n        }\n\n        const string esc = \"\\u001b\";\n        string uri = new Uri(absolutePath).AbsoluteUri;\n        return $\"{esc}]8;;{uri}{esc}\\\\{absolutePath}{esc}]8;;{esc}\\\\\";\n    }\n\n    internal static string StatusLabel(TaskOutcome outcome) => outcome switch\n    {\n        TaskOutcome.Succeeded => \"OK\",\n        TaskOutcome.Skipped => \"SKIPPED\",\n        TaskOutcome.ActionFailed => \"ACTION FAILED\",\n        TaskOutcome.GuardrailFailed => \"GUARDRAIL FAILED\",\n        TaskOutcome.InvalidFragment => \"INVALID FRAGMENT\",\n        TaskOutcome.NeedsHuman => \"NEEDS HUMAN\",\n        // Issue #190: distinct from a generic NEEDS HUMAN so the per-task summary line reads\n        // \"re-run later\", not \"something is broken here\".\n        TaskOutcome.RateLimited => \"RATE LIMITED\",\n        TaskOutcome.Blocked => \"BLOCKED\",\n        TaskOutcome.Cancelled => \"CANCELLED\",\n        _ => outcome.ToString()\n    };\n}\n","numLines":730,"startLine":1,"totalLines":730}}}

Static HTML, not templated from\n42\t/// the Mermaid source, so it carries no bearing on <c>source-sha256</c>.\n43\t/// </para>\n44\t/// <para>\n45\t/// <b>Task-container click target: a POST-RENDER SVG title-band overlay (issue #232/#233\n46\t/// superseded, issue #235).</b> A Mermaid <c>click</c> directive never fires on a subgraph/cluster\n47\t/// element (upstream limitation; see <see cref=\"MermaidRenderer\"/> remarks), and the first fix\n48\t/// attempt — an invisible anchor NODE inside the container — proved useless in practice: dagre\n49\t/// sizes and packs it into whatever gap its own layout leaves, which for a content-dense container\n50\t/// is a tiny, off-center sliver (measured: 0.44% of the container's area on a real 4-guardrail\n51\t/// task). The fix implemented here instead runs AFTER <c>mermaid.render</c> resolves: for every\n52\t/// <c>g.cluster</c> element whose id starts with <c>task_</c>, compute a full-width band from the\n53\t/// cluster's own bounding box down to just past its <c>.cluster-label</c>'s bottom edge (Mermaid\n54\t/// always reserves this header strip above any leaf content, so it is empty BY CONSTRUCTION\n55\t/// regardless of how many guardrails/preflights the task has — unlike an interior anchor node) and\n56\t/// append a real <c>&lt;a href=\"...\" target=\"_blank\"&gt;&lt;rect fill=\"transparent\"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;</c>\n57\t/// spanning it. APPENDED, not inserted first: a cluster's only two original children are its\n58\t/// background <c>&lt;rect&gt;</c> then its <c>.cluster-label</c> group, so appending puts the\n59\t/// overlay last in paint order (on top, clickable) without moving in front of the label text\n60\t/// visually (the rect is transparent) and — because every leaf check node lives in an entirely\n61\t/// separate top-level SVG group from its own container's <c>g.cluster</c> — the overlay can never\n62\t/// occlude or be occluded by a leaf node's own click target either way. The task→folder-path data\n63\t/// this script needs (<see cref=\"MermaidRenderer.TaskFolderTargets\"/>) is embedded as a small JSON\n64\t/// object keyed by the SAME container id the Mermaid source uses, in a\n65\t/// <c>&lt;script type=\"application/json\"&gt;</c> element read back via <c>textContent</c> (same\n66\t/// verbatim/never-interpolated treatment as the Mermaid source itself, immune to the same escaping\n67\t/// hazards).\n68\t/// </para>\n69\t/// <para>\n70\t/// <b>Long task-name title wrap-overflow, fixed by widening the label's foreignObject\n71\t/// post-render (issue cluster-label-wrap-overflow).</b> Mermaid always wraps a cluster's title\n72\t/// <c>&lt;div&gt;</c> to a hardcoded 200px width (the bundled <c>mermaid@11.4.1</c>'s plain\n73\t/// flowchart cluster shape never overrides <c>createText</c>'s <c>width = 200</c> default,\n74\t/// regardless of the container's own — usually much wider — computed width), and dagre's layout\n75\t/// pass reserves the container's header-strip height BEFORE that wrapped height is known, so a\n76\t/// long kebab-case task name (hyphens are valid soft line-break points) that wraps to 2+ lines\n77\t/// prints past the reserved strip, overlapping the first leaf guardrail/preflight box positioned\n78\t/// directly below. No Mermaid config knob avoids this — <c>flowchart.htmlLabels</c> only toggles\n79\t/// the html-vs-svg-text label renderer, and 11.4.1 has no documented option for cluster-label\n80\t/// wrap width. The fix (the <c>fixWrappedClusterLabels</c> function, run AFTER\n81\t/// <c>mermaid.render</c> resolves and BEFORE the title-band overlay above, since the overlay's\n82\t/// band height depends on the label's — by then possibly shrunk — bounding box) re-wraps the\n83\t/// SAME text at up to the container's own width instead: since the container is normally already\n84\t/// wider than 200px, this almost always drops the label to one line, which needs LESS height than\n85\t/// the original wrap — never more — so no other node/edge/container ever needs to move. Verified\n86\t/// headless against 8+ real plans (40+ long task names): every wrapped case converged to one line\n87\t/// with the same healthy gap a never-wrapped label already has, and every already-fits label\n88\t/// (including the whole golden <c>hello-guardrails</c> example) was left byte-for-byte unchanged.\n89\t/// </para>\n90\t/// </remarks>\n91\tpublic static class HtmlDiagramRenderer\n92\t{\n93\t    /// <summary>\n94\t    /// Build the <c>diagram.html</c> document for <paramref name=\"mermaidSource\"/> (the exact\n95\t    /// text rendered into <c>diagram.md</c>, including the <c>flowchart TD</c> header and the\n96\t    /// cosmetic <c>classDef</c> lines) stamped with <paramref name=\"sourceHash\"/>. <paramref\n97\t    /// name=\"taskFolderTargets\"/> is the task-container-id → plan-relative-folder-path map (see\n98\t    /// <see cref=\"MermaidRenderer.TaskFolderTargets\"/>) the embedded title-band overlay script uses\n99\t    /// to resolve each container's click target (issue #235 — see class remarks).\n100\t    /// </summary>\n101\t    /// <param name=\"nodeStatuses\">\n102\t    /// OPTIONAL live-run status map (issue #219): Mermaid node/container id → one of\n103\t    /// <c>\"pending\"</c>, <c>\"running\"</c>, <c>\"passed\"</c>, <c>\"failed\"</c>, <c>\"needs-human\"</c>,\n104\t    /// keyed by the SAME ids <see cref=\"MermaidRenderer\"/> emits (task containers <c>task_&lt;base&gt;</c>,\n105\t    /// preflight/guardrail leaves <c>task_&lt;base&gt;_pf_&lt;n&gt;</c>/<c>task_&lt;base&gt;_gr_&lt;n&gt;</c>,\n106\t    /// and the plan-level brackets <c>plan_preflights</c>/<c>plan_guardrails</c>). browsers block\n385\t  <code>file://&rarr;file://</code> navigation by default.\n386\t  On GitHub, diagram.md renders instead.</div>\n387\t\n388\t<script type=\"text/plain\" id=\"graph-source\">__GRAPH_SOURCE__</script>\n389\t<script type=\"application/json\" id=\"task-folder-targets\">__TASK_FOLDER_TARGETS__</script>\n390\t<script src=\"https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/svg-pan-zoom@3.6.1/dist/svg-pan-zoom.min.js\"></script>\n391\t<script type=\"module\">\n392\timport mermaid from 'https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/mermaid@11.4.1/dist/mermaid.esm.min.mjs';\n393\t\n394\tconst graph = document.getElementById('graph-source').textContent;\n395\tconst taskFolderTargets = JSON.parse(document.getElementById('task-folder-targets').textContent);\n396\t// securityLevel 'loose' is required for the `click ... href` directives to open node sources;\n397\t// the content is the user's own local plan, served from file:// — not untrusted input.\n398\t// maxTextSize raises Mermaid's default 50 000-character source ceiling (issue #108): a large\n399\t// plan's DAG source easily exceeds it, and on overflow mermaid.render throws \"Maximum text size\n400\t// in diagram exceeded\" — landing every big plan in the catch-block \"could not render\" fallback.\n401\t// 5 000 000 covers DAGs far larger than any hand-reviewed plan while still bounding the parser.\n402\t// maxEdges raises Mermaid's default 500-edge ceiling for the same reason: a many-task plan fans\n403\t// out task→guardrail→done→dependent edges well past 500, and overflow throws \"Maximum number of\n404\t// edges exceeded\". Both ceilings must be lifted or a big plan still fails to render.\n405\tmermaid.initialize({ startOnLoad: false, theme: 'dark', securityLevel: 'loose', maxTextSize: 5000000, maxEdges: 100000, flowchart: { useMaxWidth: false } });\n406\t\n407\t// Widen a wrapped cluster-title's foreignObject so a long kebab-case task name reflows onto\n408\t// FEWER lines instead of overflowing Mermaid's fixed header-strip reservation (issue\n409\t// cluster-label-wrap-overflow). Root cause, confirmed against the bundled mermaid@11.4.1 source\n410\t// (`src/rendering-util/createText.ts`'s `createText(el, text, { width = 200 })` default, which\n411\t// the plain flowchart \"rect\" cluster shape never overrides): a cluster's title <div> is always\n412\t// wrapped to a hardcoded 200px width regardless of the container's own — usually much wider —\n413\t// computed width, and dagre's LAYOUT PASS reserves header space for the container BEFORE that\n414\t// wrapped height is known, so it never grows to fit more than a couple of lines. A short label\n415\t// fits on one line inside 200px with room to spare (a healthy ~13px gap above the first leaf\n416\t// node, measured); a long name (hyphens are valid soft line-break points) wraps to 2 or more\n417\t// lines, and each line beyond the first prints a full line-height PAST what dagre reserved —\n418\t// landing on top of the first leaf guardrail/preflight box positioned directly below.\n419\t//\n420\t// Two Mermaid config knobs were checked and ruled out first: `flowchart.htmlLabels` only toggles\n421\t// the html-vs-svg-text label renderer (not label width), and 11.4.1 has no documented\n422\t// `flowchart`/theme-variable option for cluster-label wrapping width — 200 is a hardcoded\n423\t// parameter default the cluster-shape renderer never overrides, not a configurable value.\n424\t//\n425\t// The fix instead re-wraps the SAME text into fewer lines post-render: a task container is\n426\t// normally already wider than 200px (dagre sized it to fit its leaf content), so letting the\n427\t// identical label reflow at (up to) the container's own width almost always drops it to one\n428\t// line, which needs LESS height than Mermaid's original wrap — never more. That means this can\n429\t// only ever shrink the label's footprint, never grow it, so no other node/edge/container ever\n430\t// needs to move to make room. Verified headless against 8+ real plans (40+ long task names,\n431\t// container widths from ~230px to ~1100px, wraps from 2 to 3 lines): every case converged to one\n432\t// line with the same healthy negative gap a short, never-wrapped label already has; every\n433\t// already-fits-on-one-line label (including every task in the golden `hello-guardrails` example)\n434\t// was left byte-for-byte unchanged.\n435\tfunction fixWrappedClusterLabels(svgEl) {\n436\t  const clusters = svgEl.querySelectorAll('g.cluster');\n437\t  for (const cluster of clusters) {\n438\t    const label = cluster.querySelector('.cluster-label');\n439\t    if (!label) continue;\n440\t    const fo = label.querySelector('foreignObject');\n441\t    const div = fo ? fo.querySelector('div') : null;\n442\t    if (!fo || !div) continue;\n443\t\n444\t    const currentWidth = parseFloat(fo.getAttribute('width')) || 0;\n445\t    const currentHeight = parseFloat(fo.getAttribute('height')) || 0;\n446\t    if (currentWidth <= 0 || currentHeight <= 0) continue;\n447\t\n448\t    // scrollWidth/scrollHeight are LOCAL layout-box measurements in the foreignObject's own\n449\t    // SVG-user-unit coordinate system — deliberately NOT the screen-space rect API (see class\n450\t    // remarks): a screen-space read is affected by every ancestor SVG transform (dagre's per-node\n451\t    // translate, the pan-zoom viewport's zoom/pan matrix), so it would be misleading compared\n452\t    // against the foreignObject's own width/height attributes. Measure the label's natural\n453\t    // single-line width by temporarily lifting the wrap constraint Mermaid applied, then restore\n454\t    // it immediately.\n455\t    const savedInline = div.getAttribute('style');\n456\t    div.style.whiteSpace = 'nowrap';\n457\t    div.style.width = 'auto';\n458\t    div.style.maxWidth = 'none';\n459\t    // +2px safety buffer: scrollWidth can round down a hair short of the true subpixel width\n460\t    // needed for one line (observed empirically — re-applying the exact measured value as a\n461\t    // fixed width sometimes still wrapped to 2 lines by a fraction of a pixel).\n462\t    const naturalWidth = div.scrollWidth + 2;\n463\t    div.setAttribute('style', savedInline); // restore Mermaid's original wrapped styling\n464\t\n465\t    if (naturalWidth <= currentWidth) continue; // already fits on one line — untouched\n466\t\n467\t    // Never widen past the cluster's own body (leave a small margin) — the container's leaf\n468\t    // content already determined this width, so the label can use up to it without ever moving\n469\t    // a leaf node, edge, or sibling container. Never NARROWER than Mermaid's original width either\n470\t    // — this only ever grows the box.\n471\t    const clusterBox = cluster.getBBox();\n472\t    const newWidth = Math.min(naturalWidth, Math.max(currentWidth, clusterBox.width - 16));\n473\t\n474\t    div.style.whiteSpace = 'break-spaces';\n475\t    div.style.display = 'table';\n476\t    div.style.width = newWidth + 'px';\n477\t    div.style.maxWidth = newWidth + 'px';\n478\t    const newHeight = div.scrollHeight;\n479\t\n480\t    if (newHeight >= currentHeight) {\n481\t      // Widening did not actually reduce the wrapped height (the cluster is too narrow to fit\n482\t      // even one more character per line) — revert rather than risk an oddly-proportioned label.\n483\t      div.setAttribute('style', savedInline);\n484\t      continue;\n485\t    }\n486\t\n487\t    fo.setAttribute('width', String(newWidth));\n488\t    fo.setAttribute('height', String(newHeight));\n489\t\n490\t    // Mermaid centers the label group on the cluster's horizontal midpoint via\n491\t    // `translate(x - bbox.width / 2, y)`; widening the box without re-centering would shift it\n492\t    // left of center, so shift the existing translate by half the width delta to keep it on the\n493\t    // SAME midpoint. The label's y stays fixed — only its height shrank, so its bottom edge\n494\t    // simply moves UP, further from the first leaf node, never displacing it.\n495\t    const transform = label.getAttribute('transform') || '';\n496\t    const match = transform.match(/translate\\(([-\\d.eE]+)\\s*,\\s*([-\\d.eE]+)\\)/);\n497\t    if (match) {\n498\t      const oldX = parseFloat(match[1]);\n499\t      const deltaCenter = (newWidth - currentWidth) / 2;\n500\t      label.setAttribute('transform', `translate(${oldX - deltaCenter}, ${match[2]})`);\n501\t    }\n502\t  }\n503\t}\n504\t\n505\t// Inject a real clickable overlay on each task container's TITLE/LABEL ROW (issue #232/#233\n506\t// superseded, issue #235). Mermaid never fires a `click` directive on a subgraph/cluster element,\n507\t// and an anchor NODE inside the container gets packed into whatever sliver of space dagre's own\n508\t// layout leaves — useless for a content-dense container. Since this overlay rect is nested inside the .cluster group (so it stays in\n542\t    // the same paint context as the background rect it sits atop), that same selector matches it\n543\t    // too — and a CSS class/tag selector beats a plain presentation attribute, so `fill=\"transparent\"`\n544\t    // as an attribute got silently overridden, painting a solid theme-colored bar over the title text.\n545\t    // An inline style wins over any external/embedded stylesheet rule (short of `!important`), so it\n546\t    // reliably stays invisible regardless of Mermaid's theme.\n547\t    rect.style.fill = 'transparent';\n548\t    rect.style.cursor = 'pointer';\n549\t\n550\t    a.appendChild(rect);\n551\t    // Append as the LAST child — do NOT prepend as the first child. A cluster's only two original\n552\t    // children are its background <rect> then its .cluster-label group (in that paint order).\n553\t    // Appending puts the overlay on top of the background rect (so it is actually clickable)\n554\t    // without covering the label text visually (the rect is transparent either way) — prepending\n555\t    // instead would put the overlay BEHIND the background rect (which becomes second-in-order and\n556\t    // paints over it), silently blocking every click.\n557\t    cluster.appendChild(a);\n558\t  }\n559\t}\n560\t\n561\tlet pz = null;\n562\ttry {\n563\t  const { svg } = await mermaid.render('dag', graph);\n564\t  document.getElementById('stage').innerHTML = svg;\n565\t  const el = document.querySelector('#stage svg');\n566\t  el.setAttribute('width', '100vw');\n567\t  el.setAttribute('height', '100vh');\n568\t  fixWrappedClusterLabels(el);\n569\t  addTaskContainerOverlays(el);\n570\t  pz = svgPanZoom(el, { zoomEnabled: true, controlIconsEnabled: false, fit: true, center: true,\n571\t                        minZoom: 0.1, maxZoom: 20, zoomScaleSensitivity: 0.3 });\n572\t  window.addEventListener('resize', () => { pz.resize(); pz.fit(); pz.center(); });\n573\t} catch (e) {\n574\t  // Surface the actual failure (offline CDN, or a Mermaid limit we have not yet lifted) instead of\n575\t  // guessing — a generic \"offline?\" message sent every big-plan #108 failure down the wrong path.\n576\t  const detail = (e && e.message) ? includeRefresh)\n    {\n        // Same relaxed-encoder side-table treatment as taskFolderTargets: a JSON object (node id ->\n        // status word) read back via textContent from a <script content=\\\"width=device-width, initial-scale=1\\\">\\n\"\n                    + \"<meta http-equiv=\\\"refresh\\\" content=\\\"2\\\">\",\n                StringComparison.Ordinal);\n        }\n\n        // Status-badge CSS (spinner @keyframes + per-status colours) just before </style>.\n        html = html.Replace(\n            \"</style>\", StatusOverlayStyle + \"\\n</style>\", Because the badges live in\n// screen space (not inside the SVG), they are re-anchored to each node's on-screen box\n// (getBoundingClientRect) on every svg-pan-zoom pan / zoom + resize — wired below. Mirrors\n// addTaskContainerOverlays' technique: it runs on the SAME SVG mermaid.render just produced.\nconst nodeStatuses = JSON.parse(document.getElementById('node-statuses').textContent);\nlet statusBadges = [];\n\nfunction statusGlyph(status) {\n  switch (status) {\n    case 'passed': return '✓';       // U+2713 CHECK MARK\n    case 'failed': return '✗';       // U+2717 BALLOT X\n    case 'needs-human': return '?';\n    default: return '·';             // pending / blocked / anything else — a STATIC settled dot, never the spinner\n  }\n}\n\n// Resolve a status-map id to its SVG element. Each badge stays hidden\n// until positionStatusBadges places it, so a badge whose node is off-screen never flashes at (0,0).\nfunction addStatusOverlays(svgEl) {\n  let layer = document.getElementById('status-overlays');\n  if (!layer) { layer = document.createElement('div'); layer.id = 'status-overlays'; document.body.appendChild(layer); }\n  layer.innerHTML = '';\n  statusBadges = [];\n  for (const [id, status] of Object.entries(nodeStatuses)) {\n    const target = findStatusTarget(svgEl, id);\n    if (!target) continue; // id not present in this render — no badge (defensive)\n    const badge = document.createElement('div');\n    if (status === 'running') {\n      badge.className = 'status-badge status-spinner';\n    } else {\n      badge.className = 'status-badge status-' + status;\n      badge.textContent = statusGlyph(status);\n    }\n    badge.setAttribute('aria-label', id + ': ' + status);\n    badge.style.display = 'none';\n    layer.appendChild(badge);\n    statusBadges.push({ el: badge, target: target });\n  }\n  positionStatusBadges();\n}\n\n// Re-anchor every badge to the top-right corner of its node's CURRENT on-screen box. Called after the\n// overlay is built and again on every pan / zoom / resize so the badges track the diagram exactly.\nfunction positionStatusBadges() {\n  for (const b of statusBadges) {\n    const r = b.target.getBoundingClientRect();\n    if (r.width === 0 && r.height === 0) { b.el.style.display = 'none'; continue; }\n    b.el.style.display = '';\n    b.el.style.left = r.right + 'px';\n    b.el.style.top = r.top + 'px';\n  }\n}\n\"\"\";\n\n    // __SOURCE_SHA256__ is filled with a lowercase-hex hash (no escaping needed). __GRAPH_SOURCE__\n    // is filled with the verbatim Mermaid text inside a raw-text <script> element (see remarks).\n    // __TASK_FOLDER_TARGETS__ is filled with a JSON object (container id -> folder path).\n    private const string Template = \"\"\"\n<!-- guardrails:graph v1 source-sha256=__SOURCE_SHA256__ -->\n<!doctype html>\n<html lang=\"en\">\n<head>\n<meta charset=\"utf-8\">\n<meta name=\"viewport\" content=\"width=device-width, initial-scale=1\">\n<title>Guardrails — task/guardrail DAG</title>\n<style>\n  html, body { margin: 0; Both ceilings must be lifted or a big plan still fails to render.\nmermaid.initialize({ startOnLoad: false, theme: 'dark', securityLevel: 'loose', maxTextSize: 5000000, maxEdges: 100000, flowchart: { useMaxWidth: false } });\n\n// Widen a wrapped cluster-title's foreignObject so a long kebab-case task name reflows onto\n// FEWER lines instead of overflowing Mermaid's fixed header-strip reservation (issue\n// cluster-label-wrap-overflow). Root cause, confirmed against the bundled mermaid@11.4.1 source\n// (`src/rendering-util/createText.ts`'s `createText(el, text, { width = 200 })` default, which\n// the plain flowchart \"rect\" cluster shape never overrides): a cluster's title <div> is always\n// wrapped to a hardcoded 200px width regardless of the container's own — usually much wider —\n// computed width, and dagre's LAYOUT PASS reserves header space for the container BEFORE that\n// wrapped height is known, so it never grows to fit more than a couple of lines. A short label\n// fits on one line inside 200px with room to spare (a healthy ~13px gap above the first leaf\n// node, measured); a long name (hyphens are valid soft line-break points) wraps to 2 or more\n// lines, and each line beyond the first prints a full line-height PAST what dagre reserved —\n// landing on top of the first leaf guardrail/preflight box positioned directly below.\n//\n// Two Mermaid config knobs were checked and ruled out first: `flowchart.htmlLabels` only toggles\n// the html-vs-svg-text label renderer (not label width), and 11.4.1 has no documented\n// `flowchart`/theme-variable option for cluster-label wrapping width — 200 is a hardcoded\n// parameter default the cluster-shape renderer never overrides, not a configurable value.\n//\n// The fix instead re-wraps the SAME text into fewer lines post-render: a task container is\n// normally already wider than 200px (dagre sized it to fit its leaf content), so letting the\n// identical label reflow at (up to) the container's own width almost always drops it to one\n// line, which needs LESS height than Mermaid's original wrap — never more. That means this can\n// only ever shrink the label's footprint, never grow it, so no other node/edge/container ever\n// needs to move to make room. Verified headless against 8+ real plans (40+ long task names,\n// container widths from ~230px to ~1100px, wraps from 2 to 3 lines): every case converged to one\n// line with the same healthy negative gap a short, never-wrapped label already has; every\n// already-fits-on-one-line label (including every task in the golden `hello-guardrails` example)\n// was left byte-for-byte unchanged.\nfunction fixWrappedClusterLabels(svgEl) {\n  const clusters = svgEl.querySelectorAll('g.cluster');\n  for (const cluster of clusters) {\n    const label = cluster.querySelector('.cluster-label');\n    if (!label) continue;\n    const fo = label.querySelector('foreignObject');\n    const div = fo ? fo.querySelector('div') : null;\n    if (!fo || !div) continue;\n\n    const currentWidth = parseFloat(fo.getAttribute('width')) || 0;\n    const currentHeight = parseFloat(fo.getAttribute('height')) || 0;\n    if (currentWidth <= 0 || currentHeight <= 0) continue;\n\n    // scrollWidth/scrollHeight are LOCAL layout-box measurements in the foreignObject's own\n    // SVG-user-unit coordinate system — deliberately NOT the screen-space rect API (see class\n    // remarks): a screen-space read is affected by every ancestor SVG transform (dagre's per-node\n    // translate, the pan-zoom viewport's zoom/pan matrix), so it would be misleading compared\n    // against the foreignObject's own width/height attributes. Measure the label's natural\n    // single-line width by temporarily lifting the wrap constraint Mermaid applied, then restore\n    // it immediately.\n    const savedInline = div.getAttribute('style');\n    div.style.whiteSpace = 'nowrap';\n    div.style.width = 'auto';\n    div.style.maxWidth = 'none';\n    // +2px safety buffer: scrollWidth can round down a hair short of the true subpixel width\n    // needed for one line (observed empirically — re-applying the exact measured value as a\n    // fixed width sometimes still wrapped to 2 lines by a fraction of a pixel).\n    const naturalWidth = div.scrollWidth + 2;\n    div.setAttribute('style', savedInline); // restore Mermaid's original wrapped styling\n\n    if (naturalWidth <= currentWidth) continue; // already fits on one line — untouched\n\n    // Never widen past the cluster's own body (leave a small margin) — the container's leaf\n    // content already determined this width, so the label can use up to it without ever moving\n    // a leaf node, edge, or sibling container. Never NARROWER than Mermaid's original width either\n    // — this only ever grows the box.\n    const clusterBox = cluster.getBBox();\n    const newWidth = Math.min(naturalWidth, Math.max(currentWidth, clusterBox.width - 16));\n\n    div.style.whiteSpace = 'break-spaces';\n    div.style.display = 'table';\n    div.style.width = newWidth + 'px';\n    div.style.maxWidth = newWidth + 'px';\n    const newHeight = div.scrollHeight;\n\n    if (newHeight >= currentHeight) {\n      // Widening did not actually reduce the wrapped height (the cluster is too narrow to fit\n      // even one more character per line) — revert rather than risk an oddly-proportioned label.\n      div.setAttribute('style', savedInline);\n      continue;\n    }\n\n    fo.setAttribute('width', String(newWidth));\n    fo.setAttribute('height', String(newHeight));\n\n    // Mermaid centers the label group on the cluster's horizontal midpoint via\n    // `translate(x - bbox.width / 2, y)`; widening the box without re-centering would shift it\n    // left of center, so shift the existing translate by half the width delta to keep it on the\n    // SAME midpoint. The label's y stays fixed — only its height shrank, so its bottom edge\n    // simply moves UP, further from the first leaf node, never displacing it.\n    const transform = label.getAttribute('transform') || '';\n    const match = transform.match(/translate\\(([-\\d.eE]+)\\s*,\\s*([-\\d.eE]+)\\)/);\n    if (match) {\n      const oldX = parseFloat(match[1]);\n      const deltaCenter = (newWidth - currentWidth) / 2;\n      label.setAttribute('transform', `translate(${oldX - deltaCenter}, ${match[2]})`);\n    }\n  }\n}\n\n// Inject a real clickable overlay on each task container's TITLE/LABEL ROW (issue #232/#233\n// superseded, issue #235). Mermaid never fires a `click` directive on a subgraph/cluster element,\n// and an anchor NODE inside the container gets packed into whatever sliver of space dagre's own\n// layout leaves — useless for a content-dense container. The title band is different: Mermaid\n// ALWAYS renders a cluster as exactly two children, a background <rect> then a <g\n// class=\"cluster-label\">, with the label in its own reserved header strip above any leaf content —\n// so a full-width band from the cluster's top down to just past the label's bottom edge is empty BY\n// CONSTRUCTION, regardless of how many guardrails/preflights the task has. Must run AFTER\n// fixWrappedClusterLabels (above): the band height is measured from the label's CURRENT bbox, so\n// a label that fixWrappedClusterLabels already shrank back to one line gets a correspondingly\n// short band, not one sized for its original (possibly multi-line) wrap.\nfunction addTaskContainerOverlays(svgEl) {\n  const ns = 'http://www.w3.org/2000/svg';\n  const clusters = svgEl.querySelectorAll('g.cluster');\n  for (const cluster of clusters) {\n    const target = taskFolderTargets[cluster.id];\n    if (!target) continue; // not a task container (e.g. plan_preflights/plan_guardrails), or no map entry\n    const label = cluster.querySelector('.cluster-label');\n    if (!label) continue;\n\n    const clusterBox = cluster.getBBox();\n    const labelBox = label.getBBox();\n    const bandHeight = (labelBox.y + labelBox.height) - clusterBox.y + 8; // small padding below the title\n\n    const a = document.createElementNS(ns, 'a');\n    a.setAttribute('href', target);\n    a.setAttribute('target', '_blank');\n    a.setAttribute('aria-label', 'Open task folder: ' + target);\n\n    const rect = document.createElementNS(ns, 'rect');\n    rect.setAttribute('x', String(clusterBox.x));\n    rect.setAttribute('y', String(clusterBox.y));\n    rect.setAttribute('width', String(clusterBox.width));\n    rect.setAttribute('height', String(bandHeight));\n    // Inline style (not the `fill` ATTRIBUTE) deliberately: Mermaid's own generated stylesheet\n    // carries a rule like `#dag .cluster rect{fill:<theme color>;...}` that colors every cluster's\n    // background rect. Since this overlay rect is nested inside the .cluster group (so it stays in\n    // the same paint context as the background rect it sits atop), that same selector matches it\n    // too — and a CSS class/tag selector beats a plain presentation attribute, so `fill=\"transparent\"`\n    // as an attribute got silently overridden, painting a solid theme-colored bar over the title text.\n    // An inline style wins over any external/embedded stylesheet rule (short of `!important`), so it\n    // reliably stays invisible regardless of Mermaid's theme.\n    rect.style.fill = 'transparent';\n    rect.style.cursor = 'pointer';\n\n    a.appendChild(rect);\n    // Append as the LAST child — do NOT prepend as the first child. A cluster's only two original\n    // children are its background <rect> then its .cluster-label group (in that paint order).\n    // Appending puts the overlay on top of the background rect (so it is actually clickable)\n    // without covering the label text visually (the rect is transparent either way) — prepending\n    // instead would put the overlay BEHIND the background rect (which becomes second-in-order and\n    // paints over it), silently blocking every click.\n    cluster.appendChild(a);\n  }\n}\n\nlet pz = null;\ntry {\n  const { svg } = await mermaid.render('dag', graph);\n  document.getElementById('stage').innerHTML = svg;\n  const el = document.querySelector('#stage svg');\n  el.setAttribute('width', '100vw');\n  el.setAttribute('height', '100vh');\n  fixWrappedClusterLabels(el);\n  addTaskContainerOverlays(el);\n  pz = svgPanZoom(el, { zoomEnabled: true, controlIconsEnabled: false, fit: true, center: true,\n                        minZoom: 0.1, maxZoom: 20, zoomScaleSensitivity: 0.3 });\n  window.addEventListener('resize', () => { pz.resize(); pz.fit(); pz.center(); });\n} catch (e) {\n  // Surface the actual failure (offline CDN, or a Mermaid limit we have not yet lifted) instead of\n  // guessing — a generic \"offline?\" message sent every big-plan #108 failure down the wrong path.\n  const detail = (e && e.message) ? String(e.message) : String(e);\n  const p = document.createElement('p');\n  p.style.padding = '1rem';\n  p.textContent = 'Could not render the diagram: ' + detail +\n    ' — the DAG source is embedded in this page, and diagram.md renders on GitHub.';\n  const stage = document.getElementById('stage');\n  stage.innerHTML = '';\n  stage.appendChild(p);\n}\n\ndocument.getElementById('fit').onclick  = () => { if (pz) { pz.fit(); pz.center(); } };\ndocument.getElementById('zin').onclick  = () => { if (pz) pz.zoomBy(1.3); };\ndocument.getElementById('zout').onclick = () => { if (pz) pz.zoomBy(0.77); };\ndocument.getElementById('fs').onclick   = () => document.documentElement.requestFullscreen();\n</script>\n</body>\n</html>\n\"\"\";\n}\n","numLines":595,"startLine":1,"totalLines":595}}}

GraphSourceHash.Compute(plan);\n        string diagramPath = Path.Combine(plan.PlanDirectory, DiagramFileName);\n        string diagramHtmlPath = Path.Combine(plan.PlanDirectory, DiagramHtmlFileName);\n\n        if (check)\n        {\n            return Check(diagramPath, diagramHtmlPath, sourceHash, output);\n        }\n\n        string diagram = MermaidRenderer.Render(plan);\n\n        if (toStdout)\n        {\n            output.WriteLine(diagram);\n            return ExitCodes.Success;\n        }\n\n        string document = ComposeDocument(diagram, sourceHash);\n        AtomicFile.WriteAllText(diagramPath, document);\n        output.WriteLine($\"Wrote {diagramPath}\");\n\n        // The interactive local-navigation companion (issue #33). diagram.md stays the GitHub\n        // render; diagram.html is the pan/zoom/fullscreen viewer whose nodes click through to\n        // their source under the plan folder. Both carry the same source-sha256 and are excluded\n        // from guardrails.baseline, so neither causes drift. The HTML embeds the interactive\n        // source (clean diagram + click directives); diagram.md stays click-free.\n        if (!noHtml)\n        {\n            string interactive = MermaidRenderer.RenderInteractive(plan);\n            IReadOnlyDictionary<string, string> taskFolderTargets = MermaidRenderer.TaskFolderTargets(plan);\n            AtomicFile.WriteAllText(diagramHtmlPath, HtmlDiagramRenderer.Render(interactive, sourceHash, taskFolderTargets));\n            output.WriteLine($\"Wrote {diagramHtmlPath}\");\n        }\n\n        return ExitCodes.Success;\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// <c>--check</c>: recompute the source hash and compare it to the one embedded in an\n    /// existing <c>diagram.md</c> provenance comment. Fresh (present and equal) → exit 0;\n    /// stale or missing → one actionable line on stdout and exit <see cref=\"StaleExitCode\"/>\n    /// (2) — the \"regenerate\" signal (SSOT §7/§10). A genuine load/validate failure never\n    /// reaches here — <see cref=\"Execute\"/> returns <see cref=\"ExitCodes.HarnessError\"/> (1)\n    /// before <c>--check</c> is dispatched — so CI can distinguish \"regenerate the diagram\"\n    /// (2) from \"the plan is broken\" (1).\n    /// </summary>\n    private static int Check(string diagramPath, string diagramHtmlPath, string sourceHash, TextWriter output)\n    {\n        string regenHint = $\"run: guardrails graph {QuoteIfNeeded(Path.GetDirectoryName(diagramPath)!)}\";\n\n        if (!File.Exists(diagramPath))\n        {\n            output.WriteLine($\"{DiagramFileName} missing — {regenHint}\");\n            return StaleExitCode;\n        }\n\n        if (!string.Equals(ReadEmbeddedHash(File.ReadAllText(diagramPath)), sourceHash, StringComparison.Ordinal))\n        {\n            output.WriteLine($\"{DiagramFileName} is stale — {regenHint}\");\n            return StaleExitCode;\n        }\n\n        // diagram.html is optional (it is skipped by --no-html), so a MISSING one is not staleness.\n        // But a PRESENT one carrying a different source-sha256 has drifted from diagram.md/the plan\n        // and must regenerate — it shares the same staleness key (issue #33).\n        if (File.Exists(diagramHtmlPath) &&\n            !string.Equals(ReadEmbeddedHash(File.ReadAllText(diagramHtmlPath)), sourceHash, StringComparison.Ordinal))\n        {\n            output.WriteLine($\"{DiagramHtmlFileName} is stale — {regenHint}\");\n            return StaleExitCode;\n        }\n\n        return ExitCodes.Success;\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// One-line italic caption written AFTER the closing mermaid fence (SSOT §10). It lives in\n    /// the markdown wrapper ONLY — never inside the fenced block, the <see cref=\"MermaidRenderer\"/>\n    /// output, or the hashed semantic content — so it does NOT affect <c>source-sha256</c>, the\n    /// golden render test, or <c>--stdout</c>, and two regens stay byte-identical. Its job is to\n    /// tell a reader the diagram is structure-only: retry, feedback, and needs-human edges are\n    /// out of scope for v1 (SSOT §10) and the static flowchart would otherwise read like a\n    /// one-pass pipeline.\n    /// </summary>\n    private const string DiagramCaption =\n        \"_Structure only — retry, feedback, and needs-human edges are omitted._\";\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Compose the persisted artifact: a single-line provenance comment (SSOT §10), a fenced\n    /// <c>mermaid</c> block holding the rendered diagram, a one-line italic caption after the\n    /// fence (<see cref=\"DiagramCaption\"/>), and the shared <see cref=\"MermaidRenderer.LegendMarkdown\"/>\n    /// block. GitHub's Mermaid sandbox has no overlay-content option, so the legend cannot live\n    /// inside the fenced block (a Mermaid-native legend subgraph was prototyped and rendered\n    /// broken — see <see cref=\"MermaidRenderer\"/> remarks); a plain Markdown block after the fence\n    /// is the only placement that reads correctly on GitHub. The comment carries only the\n    /// <c>source-sha256</c> identity — no timestamp — and both the caption and the legend are\n    /// outside the hashed content, so re-running <c>graph</c> on an unchanged plan yields a\n    /// byte-identical file (a deterministic projection, no git churn) and legend wording changes\n    /// never move <c>source-sha256</c>.\n    /// </summary>\n    private static string ComposeDocument(string diagram, string sourceHash)\n    {\n        string provenance = $\"<!-- guardrails:graph v1 source-sha256={sourceHash} -->\";\n\n        return provenance + \"\\n\\n```mermaid\\n\" + diagram.TrimEnd('\\n') + \"\\n```\\n\\n\" + DiagramCaption + \"\\n\\n\"\n            + MermaidRenderer.LegendMarkdown;\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Parse the <c>source-sha256</c> token from the provenance comment, or null if no\n    /// recognizable provenance line is present. The regex is anchored to the START of the\n    /// document (SSOT §10: the provenance is the first line), so body text echoed into the\n    /// mermaid block (e.g. a description) can never be matched as the embedded hash.\n    /// </summary>\n    private static string? ReadEmbeddedHash(string document)\n    {\n        Match match = ProvenanceHashRegex().Match(document);\n        return match.Success ? match.Groups[\"hash\"].Value : null;\n    }\n\n    private static string QuoteIfNeeded(string path) =>\n        path.Contains(' ', StringComparison.Ordinal) ? $\"\\\"{path}\\\"\" : path;\n\n    [GeneratedRegex(@\"\\A\\s*<!--\\s*guardrails:graph\\s+v1\\s+source-sha256=(?<hash>[0-9a-f]+)\\b\")]\n    private static partial Regex ProvenanceHashRegex();\n}\n","numLines":220,"startLine":1,"totalLines":220}}}
